I get this physical pressure in my chest that drives me to start planning and organizing immediately, and it feels impossible to slow down or think about the consequences until afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you also experience periods where your mood shifts to feeling extremely low, sad, or hopeless, or is it mostly this high-energy, excited state that you've been describing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt low or sad at all; it's been almost entirely this buzzing, high-energy state where I feel on top of the world. However, I do get highly wound up and swing into feeling very irritable and frustrated when people try to slow me down or question my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how long these high-energy episodes typically last, and do you experience a return to a normal mood in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This high-energy state has been going on continuously for about a month now, starting right around the time my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date. Before this past month, I was always my usual warm, steady self, so this is actually the very first time I've ever experienced anything like this.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state started around the time your supervisor pushed you to set a firm retirement date, but you've had it continuously for a month; can you think of any other significant life events or stressors that might have contributed to this change, or was it really just this one event that seems to have triggered it?\n\nPatient: Yes, around that same time, several of my younger relatives moved away for work. I felt this sudden, strong pressure to make up for their absence, which drove me to start frantically organizing more church events, youth mentoring, and family gatherings to fill the gap.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but can you recall if there have been any brief periods, even just a day or two, where you felt your normal self again before the high energy resumed?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any breaks or normal days at all. It has been completely non-stop, 24/7 high energy and planning since my supervisor pushed for that retirement date. I haven't had a single day this past month where I felt like my usual, steady self.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your spending habits or engaging in impulsive buying sprees during this high-energy period?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve been volunteering the little savings my wife and I have to fund these big events and projects I suddenly dream up. I've also promised to host huge gatherings and fix up people's houses, even though our bank account really can't handle it. It's only afterward that I realize how risky and unfair all of this is to my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted less than a month, or has this been a single, continuous episode since it started?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had anything like this happen before in my life. This has been one single, continuous episode that started about a month ago, right after my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date."

I get this physical pressure in my chest that drives me to start planning and organizing immediately, and it feels impossible to slow down or think about the consequences until afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you also experience periods where your mood shifts to feeling extremely low, sad, or hopeless, or is it mostly this high-energy, excited state that you've been describing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt low or sad at all; it's been almost entirely this buzzing, high-energy state where I feel on top of the world. However, I do get highly wound up and swing into feeling very irritable and frustrated when people try to slow me down or question my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how long these high-energy episodes typically last, and do you experience a return to a normal mood in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This high-energy state has been going on continuously for about a month now, starting right around the time my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date. Before this past month, I was always my usual warm, steady self, so this is actually the very first time I've ever experienced anything like this.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state started around the time your supervisor pushed you to set a firm retirement date, but you've had it continuously for a month; can you think of any other significant life events or stressors that might have contributed to this change, or was it really just this one event that seems to have triggered it?\n\nPatient: Yes, around that same time, several of my younger relatives moved away for work. I felt this sudden, strong pressure to make up for their absence, which drove me to start frantically organizing more church events, youth mentoring, and family gatherings to fill the gap.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but can you recall if there have been any brief periods, even just a day or two, where you felt your normal self again before the high energy resumed?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any breaks or normal days at all. It has been completely non-stop, 24/7 high energy and planning since my supervisor pushed for that retirement date. I haven't had a single day this past month where I felt like my usual, steady self.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your spending habits or engaging in impulsive buying sprees during this high-energy period?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve been volunteering the little savings my wife and I have to fund these big events and projects I suddenly dream up. I've also promised to host huge gatherings and fix up people's houses, even though our bank account really can't handle it. It's only afterward that I realize how risky and unfair all of this is to my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted less than a month, or has this been a single, continuous episode since it started?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had anything like this happen before in my life. This has been one single, continuous episode that started about a month ago, right after my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of high energy, have you experienced any thoughts or beliefs that seem unreal or detached from reality, such as hearing voices or feeling like you have special powers?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't heard any voices or believed that I have special powers. While I sometimes feel on top of the world, like I'm the only one who really knows what needs to be done around here, I haven't felt detached from reality in that way."

My wife and kids got really worried after I took a long drive recently without telling anyone, so they insisted I come in to get checked out.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happens before you jump into these big plans, do you feel an overwhelming urge or excitement that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—I wake up after just a few hours of sleep feeling on top of the world and buzzing with an intense energy that has me pacing the floors. However, I do get highly wound up and swing into feeling very irritable and frustrated when people try to slow me down or question my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how long these high-energy episodes typically last, and do you experience a return to a normal mood in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This high-energy state has been going on continuously for about a month now, starting right around the time my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date. I've also promised to host huge gatherings and fix up people's houses, even though our bank account really can't handle it. It's only afterward that I realize how risky and unfair all of this is to my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted less than a month, or has this been a single, continuous episode since it started?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had anything like this happen before in my life. This has been one single, continuous episode that started about a month ago, right after my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of high energy, have you experienced any thoughts or beliefs that seem unreal or detached from reality, such as hearing voices or feeling like you have special powers?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't heard any voices or believed that I have special powers. While I sometimes feel on top of the world, like I'm the only one who really knows what needs to be done around here, I haven't felt detached from reality in that way.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but to clarify, have you had any periods where the intensity of this energy and these plans decreased slightly, even if the overall state remained elevated?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really noticed any clear drops in my energy, as this past month has just felt like one continuous, non-stop blur.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like you're on top of the world and that you're the only one who really knows what needs to be done, can you tell me more about what you mean by that, do you feel like you have an increased sense of self-importance or grandeur?"

I get this physical pressure in my chest that drives me to start planning and organizing immediately, and it feels impossible to slow down or think about the consequences until afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you also experience periods where your mood shifts to feeling extremely low, sad, or hopeless, or is it mostly this high-energy, excited state that you've been describing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt low or sad at all; it's been almost entirely this buzzing, high-energy state where I feel on top of the world. However, I do get highly wound up and swing into feeling very irritable and frustrated when people try to slow me down or question my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how long these high-energy episodes typically last, and do you experience a return to a normal mood in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This high-energy state has been going on continuously for about a month now, starting right around the time my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date. Before this past month, I was always my usual warm, steady self, so this is actually the very first time I've ever experienced anything like this.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state started around the time your supervisor pushed you to set a firm retirement date, but you've had it continuously for a month; can you think of any other significant life events or stressors that might have contributed to this change, or was it really just this one event that seems to have triggered it?\n\nPatient: Yes, around that same time, several of my younger relatives moved away for work. I felt this sudden, strong pressure to make up for their absence, which drove me to start frantically organizing more church events, youth mentoring, and family gatherings to fill the gap.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but can you recall if there have been any brief periods, even just a day or two, where you felt your normal self again before the high energy resumed?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any breaks or normal days at all. It has been completely non-stop, 24/7 high energy and planning since my supervisor pushed for that retirement date. I haven't had a single day this past month where I felt like my usual, steady self.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your spending habits or engaging in impulsive buying sprees during this high-energy period?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve been volunteering the little savings my wife and I have to fund these big events and projects I suddenly dream up. While I sometimes feel on top of the world, like I'm the only one who really knows what needs to be done around here, I haven't felt detached from reality in that way.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but to clarify, have you had any periods where the intensity of this energy and these plans decreased slightly, even if the overall state remained elevated?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really noticed any clear drops in my energy, as this past month has just felt like one continuous, non-stop blur.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like you're on top of the world and that you're the only one who really knows what needs to be done, can you tell me more about what you mean by that, do you feel like you have an increased sense of self-importance or grandeur?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had this intense rush of confidence, feeling like I have the perfect vision for our church projects and that I'm the only one who can get them done right. Because of that, I get highly frustrated and snap at my wife or the pastor over the smallest pushback, even though I later regret being so irritable.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a specific situation where you felt like your ideas or plans were so superior that you became frustrated or angry with others for not immediately agreeing with you?"

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: Lately I keep jumping into big plans and trips without thinking, and it’s starting to cause problems for my family. My wife and kids got really worried after I took a long drive recently without telling anyone, so they insisted I come in to get checked out.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happens before you jump into these big plans, do you feel an overwhelming urge or excitement that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—I wake up after just a few hours of sleep feeling on top of the world and buzzing with an intense energy that has me pacing the floors. I get this physical pressure in my chest that drives me to start planning and organizing immediately, and it feels impossible to slow down or think about the consequences until afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you also experience periods where your mood shifts to feeling extremely low, sad, or hopeless, or is it mostly this high-energy, excited state that you've been describing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt low or sad at all; it's been almost entirely this buzzing, high-energy state where I feel on top of the world. However, I do get highly wound up and swing into feeling very irritable and frustrated when people try to slow me down or question my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how long these high-energy episodes typically last, and do you experience a return to a normal mood in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This high-energy state has been going on continuously for about a month now, starting right around the time my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date. Before this past month, I was always my usual warm, steady self, so this is actually the very first time I've ever experienced anything like this.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state started around the time your supervisor pushed you to set a firm retirement date, but you've had it continuously for a month; can you think of any other significant life events or stressors that might have contributed to this change, or was it really just this one event that seems to have triggered it?\n\nPatient: Yes, around that same time, several of my younger relatives moved away for work. I felt this sudden, strong pressure to make up for their absence, which drove me to start frantically organizing more church events, youth mentoring, and family gatherings to fill the gap.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but can you recall if there have been any brief periods, even just a day or two, where you felt your normal self again before the high energy resumed?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any breaks or normal days at all. It has been completely non-stop, 24/7 high energy and planning since my supervisor pushed for that retirement date. I haven't had a single day this past month where I felt like my usual, steady self.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your spending habits or engaging in impulsive buying sprees during this high-energy period?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve been volunteering the little savings my wife and I have to fund these big events and projects I suddenly dream up. I've also promised to host huge gatherings and fix up people's houses, even though our bank account really can't handle it. It's only afterward that I realize how risky and unfair all of this is to my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted less than a month, or has this been a single, continuous episode since it started?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had anything like this happen before in my life. This has been one single, continuous episode that started about a month ago, right after my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of high energy, have you experienced any thoughts or beliefs that seem unreal or detached from reality, such as hearing voices or feeling like you have special powers?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't heard any voices or believed that I have special powers. While I sometimes feel on top of the world, like I'm the only one who really knows what needs to be done around here, I haven't felt detached from reality in that way.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but to clarify, have you had any periods where the intensity of this energy and these plans decreased slightly, even if the overall state remained elevated?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really noticed any clear drops in my energy, as this past month has just felt like one continuous, non-stop blur.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like you're on top of the world and that you're the only one who really knows what needs to be done, can you tell me more about what you mean by that, do you feel like you have an increased sense of self-importance or grandeur?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had this intense rush of confidence, feeling like I have the perfect vision for our church projects and that I'm the only one who can get them done right. Because of that, I get highly frustrated and snap at my wife or the pastor over the smallest pushback, even though I later regret being so irritable.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a specific situation where you felt like your ideas or plans were so superior that you became frustrated or angry with others for not immediately agreeing with you?\n\nPatient: Yes, recently when I tried to push some new church projects, my pastor and my wife suggested we slow down, and I snapped at them because I felt like I was the only one who knew what really needed to be done. I got highly frustrated over that tiny bit of pushback and found it incredibly hard to dial it down, even though I could see I was upsetting them. It's just very hard to stop when I'm so convinced my plans are right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social or occupational functioning, or required hospitalization, due to your high-energy episodes and impulsive behaviors?"

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: Lately I keep jumping into big plans and trips without thinking, and it’s starting to cause problems for my family. My wife and kids got really worried after I took a long drive recently without telling anyone, so they insisted I come in to get checked out.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happens before you jump into these big plans, do you feel an overwhelming urge or excitement that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—I wake up after just a few hours of sleep feeling on top of the world and buzzing with an intense energy that has me pacing the floors. I get this physical pressure in my chest that drives me to start planning and organizing immediately, and it feels impossible to slow down or think about the consequences until afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you also experience periods where your mood shifts to feeling extremely low, sad, or hopeless, or is it mostly this high-energy, excited state that you've been describing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt low or sad at all; it's been almost entirely this buzzing, high-energy state where I feel on top of the world. However, I do get highly wound up and swing into feeling very irritable and frustrated when people try to slow me down or question my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how long these high-energy episodes typically last, and do you experience a return to a normal mood in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This high-energy state has been going on continuously for about a month now, starting right around the time my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date. Before this past month, I was always my usual warm, steady self, so this is actually the very first time I've ever experienced anything like this.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state started around the time your supervisor pushed you to set a firm retirement date, but you've had it continuously for a month; can you think of any other significant life events or stressors that might have contributed to this change, or was it really just this one event that seems to have triggered it?\n\nPatient: Yes, around that same time, several of my younger relatives moved away for work. I felt this sudden, strong pressure to make up for their absence, which drove me to start frantically organizing more church events, youth mentoring, and family gatherings to fill the gap.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but can you recall if there have been any brief periods, even just a day or two, where you felt your normal self again before the high energy resumed?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any breaks or normal days at all. It has been completely non-stop, 24/7 high energy and planning since my supervisor pushed for that retirement date. I haven't had a single day this past month where I felt like my usual, steady self.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your spending habits or engaging in impulsive buying sprees during this high-energy period?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve been volunteering the little savings my wife and I have to fund these big events and projects I suddenly dream up. I've also promised to host huge gatherings and fix up people's houses, even though our bank account really can't handle it. It's only afterward that I realize how risky and unfair all of this is to my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted less than a month, or has this been a single, continuous episode since it started?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had anything like this happen before in my life. This has been one single, continuous episode that started about a month ago, right after my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of high energy, have you experienced any thoughts or beliefs that seem unreal or detached from reality, such as hearing voices or feeling like you have special powers?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't heard any voices or believed that I have special powers. While I sometimes feel on top of the world, like I'm the only one who really knows what needs to be done around here, I haven't felt detached from reality in that way.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but to clarify, have you had any periods where the intensity of this energy and these plans decreased slightly, even if the overall state remained elevated?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really noticed any clear drops in my energy, as this past month has just felt like one continuous, non-stop blur.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like you're on top of the world and that you're the only one who really knows what needs to be done, can you tell me more about what you mean by that, do you feel like you have an increased sense of self-importance or grandeur?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had this intense rush of confidence, feeling like I have the perfect vision for our church projects and that I'm the only one who can get them done right. Because of that, I get highly frustrated and snap at my wife or the pastor over the smallest pushback, even though I later regret being so irritable.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a specific situation where you felt like your ideas or plans were so superior that you became frustrated or angry with others for not immediately agreeing with you?\n\nPatient: Yes, recently when I tried to push some new church projects, my pastor and my wife suggested we slow down, and I snapped at them because I felt like I was the only one who knew what really needed to be done. I got highly frustrated over that tiny bit of pushback and found it incredibly hard to dial it down, even though I could see I was upsetting them. It's just very hard to stop when I'm so convinced my plans are right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social or occupational functioning, or required hospitalization, due to your high-energy episodes and impulsive behaviors?\n\nPatient: I haven't been hospitalized, but this has severely strained my commitments and my relationships. I've missed several mentoring sessions, upset church leaders by barging in with half-baked plans, and my wife says she can no longer rely on me or feel safe after I disappeared on those sudden solo drives.\n\nDoctor: You've described a continuous month of high energy without a break, but can you recall if there were any brief moments, perhaps just a few hours, where your energy levels or mood seemed to shift or normalize before returning to this high state?"

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: Lately I keep jumping into big plans and trips without thinking, and it’s starting to cause problems for my family. My wife and kids got really worried after I took a long drive recently without telling anyone, so they insisted I come in to get checked out.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happens before you jump into these big plans, do you feel an overwhelming urge or excitement that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—I wake up after just a few hours of sleep feeling on top of the world and buzzing with an intense energy that has me pacing the floors. I get this physical pressure in my chest that drives me to start planning and organizing immediately, and it feels impossible to slow down or think about the consequences until afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you also experience periods where your mood shifts to feeling extremely low, sad, or hopeless, or is it mostly this high-energy, excited state that you've been describing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt low or sad at all; it's been almost entirely this buzzing, high-energy state where I feel on top of the world. However, I do get highly wound up and swing into feeling very irritable and frustrated when people try to slow me down or question my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how long these high-energy episodes typically last, and do you experience a return to a normal mood in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This high-energy state has been going on continuously for about a month now, starting right around the time my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date. Before this past month, I was always my usual warm, steady self, so this is actually the very first time I've ever experienced anything like this.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state started around the time your supervisor pushed you to set a firm retirement date, but you've had it continuously for a month; can you think of any other significant life events or stressors that might have contributed to this change, or was it really just this one event that seems to have triggered it?\n\nPatient: Yes, around that same time, several of my younger relatives moved away for work. I felt this sudden, strong pressure to make up for their absence, which drove me to start frantically organizing more church events, youth mentoring, and family gatherings to fill the gap.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but can you recall if there have been any brief periods, even just a day or two, where you felt your normal self again before the high energy resumed?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any breaks or normal days at all. It has been completely non-stop, 24/7 high energy and planning since my supervisor pushed for that retirement date. I haven't had a single day this past month where I felt like my usual, steady self.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your spending habits or engaging in impulsive buying sprees during this high-energy period?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve been volunteering the little savings my wife and I have to fund these big events and projects I suddenly dream up. I've also promised to host huge gatherings and fix up people's houses, even though our bank account really can't handle it. It's only afterward that I realize how risky and unfair all of this is to my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted less than a month, or has this been a single, continuous episode since it started?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had anything like this happen before in my life. This has been one single, continuous episode that started about a month ago, right after my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of high energy, have you experienced any thoughts or beliefs that seem unreal or detached from reality, such as hearing voices or feeling like you have special powers?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't heard any voices or believed that I have special powers. While I sometimes feel on top of the world, like I'm the only one who really knows what needs to be done around here, I haven't felt detached from reality in that way.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this high-energy state has been continuous for about a month, but to clarify, have you had any periods where the intensity of this energy and these plans decreased slightly, even if the overall state remained elevated?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really noticed any clear drops in my energy, as this past month has just felt like one continuous, non-stop blur.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like you're on top of the world and that you're the only one who really knows what needs to be done, can you tell me more about what you mean by that, do you feel like you have an increased sense of self-importance or grandeur?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had this intense rush of confidence, feeling like I have the perfect vision for our church projects and that I'm the only one who can get them done right. Because of that, I get highly frustrated and snap at my wife or the pastor over the smallest pushback, even though I later regret being so irritable.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a specific situation where you felt like your ideas or plans were so superior that you became frustrated or angry with others for not immediately agreeing with you?\n\nPatient: Yes, recently when I tried to push some new church projects, my pastor and my wife suggested we slow down, and I snapped at them because I felt like I was the only one who knew what really needed to be done. I got highly frustrated over that tiny bit of pushback and found it incredibly hard to dial it down, even though I could see I was upsetting them. It's just very hard to stop when I'm so convinced my plans are right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social or occupational functioning, or required hospitalization, due to your high-energy episodes and impulsive behaviors?\n\nPatient: I haven't been hospitalized, but this has severely strained my commitments and my relationships. I've missed several mentoring sessions, upset church leaders by barging in with half-baked plans, and my wife says she can no longer rely on me or feel safe after I disappeared on those sudden solo drives.\n\nDoctor: You've described a continuous month of high energy without a break, but can you recall if there were any brief moments, perhaps just a few hours, where your energy levels or mood seemed to shift or normalize before returning to this high state?\n\nPatient: I don't really recall any moments where my energy normalized or went back to my usual self. It has just felt like a continuous, non-stop blur of high energy for the entire past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm again if you've had any periods, no matter how brief, where you felt a noticeable decrease in your energy levels or a return to a more normal mood, or has it been a completely consistent high-energy state for the entire month?"
  }

I haven't really noticed any clear drops in my energy, as this past month has just felt like one continuous, non-stop blur.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like you're on top of the world and that you're the only one who really knows what needs to be done, can you tell me more about what you mean by that, do you feel like you have an increased sense of self-importance or grandeur?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had this intense rush of confidence, feeling like I have the perfect vision for our church projects and that I'm the only one who can get them done right. Because of that, I get highly frustrated and snap at my wife or the pastor over the smallest pushback, even though I later regret being so irritable.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a specific situation where you felt like your ideas or plans were so superior that you became frustrated or angry with others for not immediately agreeing with you?\n\nPatient: Yes, recently when I tried to push some new church projects, my pastor and my wife suggested we slow down, and I snapped at them because I felt like I was the only one who knew what really needed to be done. I got highly frustrated over that tiny bit of pushback and found it incredibly hard to dial it down, even though I could see I was upsetting them. It's just very hard to stop when I'm so convinced my plans are right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social or occupational functioning, or required hospitalization, due to your high-energy episodes and impulsive behaviors?\n\nPatient: I haven't been hospitalized, but this has severely strained my commitments and my relationships. I've missed several mentoring sessions, upset church leaders by barging in with half-baked plans, and my wife says she can no longer rely on me or feel safe after I disappeared on those sudden solo drives.\n\nDoctor: You've described a continuous month of high energy without a break, but can you recall if there were any brief moments, perhaps just a few hours, where your energy levels or mood seemed to shift or normalize before returning to this high state?\n\nPatient: I don't really recall any moments where my energy normalized or went back to my usual self. It has just felt like a continuous, non-stop blur of high energy for the entire past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm again if you've had any periods, no matter how brief, where you felt a noticeable decrease in your energy levels or a return to a more normal mood, or has it been a completely consistent high-energy state for the entire month?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been completely consistent for the entire month without any breaks or returns to normal. I feel constantly buzzing with energy day and night, and even when my mood swings, it only goes between feeling on top of the world and feeling highly irritable, never returning to a calm state."

Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).     "S034": "The patient describes experiencing a 'completely continuous, non-stop buzz of high energy for the entire month,' which directly indicates abnormally and persistently increased energy."
